Here’s how I’d think about it. **ComfyScript** ComfyScript should not be the center of VibeComfy. It should be treated as a reference adapter / compatibility source. Useful roles: - Learn from it: inspect how it represents Comfy graphs in Python. - Import from it if users already have ComfyScript examples. - Potentially export to it later if that format proves useful. - Use its example workflows as part of the workflow sample corpus. But VibeComfy’s core should be its own Python scratchpad abstraction, because the thing we need is agent-friendly editing, validation, execution, and repair. If ComfyScript’s API shape does not map cleanly to that, we should not contort around it. So in the process: ```text Workflow sources: - Official Comfy workflow templates - External example workflows - ComfyScript examples, where useful VibeComfy core: - Convert source workflow -> VibeWorkflow - Edit/compose in Python - Compile to Comfy API dict - Execute ``` ComfyScript belongs on the **input/reference edge**, not as the core runtime model. **LLM / Agent Layer** I agree the LLM layer is really “you talking to an agent that edits and runs Python,” not a product UI layer. So the right abstraction is not a visual tool stack. It is a set of agent-friendly commands and Python APIs that let an agent quickly answer: 1. What workflows exist? 2. What does this workflow need? 3. What can I safely edit? 4. Can I convert it into VibeComfy format? 5. Can I run it? 6. What broke? 7. What files/media/logs were produced? The agent-facing surface should probably be: ```bash vibecomfy sources sync vibecomfy list vibecomfy inspect <workflow> vibecomfy convert <workflow> --out scratchpads/foo.py vibecomfy run scratchpads/foo.py vibecomfy validate scratchpads/foo.py vibecomfy doctor <workflow-or-run-id> vibecomfy logs <run-id> ``` And in Python: ```python from vibecomfy import WorkflowLibrary, Scratchpad, run library = WorkflowLibrary.load() template = library.get("wan_txt2video") pad = Scratchpad.from_template(template) pad.replace_prompt("...") pad.add_node(...) pad.connect(...) result = await run(pad) ``` The key is that the agent edits normal Python files, not opaque JSON blobs. JSON can still exist underneath, but the agent’s working surface should be code. **Configuration Layer** This should probably move earlier in the plan. Before serious workflow execution, we need a reproducible runtime profile: ```text RuntimeProfile: python version comfy package version torch/cuda version device VRAM class model paths custom_nodes paths output path runpod storage mount ``` That gives the agent a way to ask: ```bash vibecomfy doctor runtime vibecomfy doctor nodes vibecomfy doctor […]

But reliability needs a coverage system, not just ad hoc conversion. The right shape is a curated workflow corpus directory plus a manifest: ```text workflow_corpus/ official/ image/ sd15_txt2img.json sdxl_txt2img.json sdxl_refiner.json flux_schnell_txt2img.json flux_dev_img2img.json flux_fill_inpaint.json video/ wan_t2v.json wan_i2v.json ltxv_t2v.json ltxv_i2v.json hunyuan_video.json image_edit/ qwen_image_edit.json kontext_edit.json openai_image_inpaint.json audio/ ace_step_t2a.json 3d/ hunyuan3d.json custom_nodes/ kijai_kjnodes/ resize_saveimage.json kijai_wan_wrapper/ wan_t2v.json wan_i2v_controlnet.json video_helpers/ save_webm.json manifests/ coverage.yaml model_requirements.yaml node_requirements.yaml ``` Each workflow should have metadata: ```yaml id: wan_t2v_official source: official path: official/video/wan_t2v.json model_family: wan task: text_to-video media: video required_nodes: - ComfyUI core required_models: - wan diffusion model - text encoder - vae coverage_tier: required validation: convert: required validate: required run_baseline: required run_vibecomfy: required output_sanity: mp4_nonempty ``` Then CI/RunPod should produce a matrix like: ```text workflow id | source | convert | validate | baseline run | vibecomfy run | output sanity | time | failure ``` **How We Make Conversion Reliable** We need four gates: 1. **Shape normalization** Detect UI graph vs API dict. Convert both into one internal `VibeWorkflow`. 2. **Requirement analysis** Before running, report missing models, missing custom nodes, unsupported node classes, and bad input files. 3. **Round-trip compilation** For every corpus workflow: ```bash vibecomfy convert <workflow.json> --out out/scratchpads/<id>.py vibecomfy validate out/scratchpads/<id>.py vibecomfy run out/scratchpads/<id>.py ``` 4. **Output sanity** Not just “process exited 0”. Check image dimensions/stddev, video frame count/duration, audio RMS, 3D file size/loadability. **Major Workflow Coverage** The practical coverage list should be task-first, then model-family: ```text Text to image: - SD 1.5 - SDXL - SDXL + refiner - FLUX Schnell - FLUX Dev / Kontext Image edit / inpaint: - SDXL inpaint - FLUX Fill - Qwen Image Edit - OpenAI image inpaint API workflow Video: - Wan T2V - Wan I2V - Wan control / inpaint where available - LTXV T2V - LTXV I2V - HunyuanVideo 3D: - Hunyuan3D Audio: - ACE Step / T2A Custom-node compatibility: - KJNodes - WanVideoWrapper - Video output helpers ``` **Source Strategy** Use official templates as the base because they are the canonical high-signal corpus. Then scan installed custom-node folders for `example_workflows/`, which Comfy documents as the standard convention for custom node workflow templates: https://docs.comfy.org/custom-nodes/workflow_templates For prominent custom-node sources, use Comfy Registry / Manager as the discovery layer, then pin repos by SHA: https://docs.comfy.org/registry/overview Current state: official image + official Wan passed end to […]

and is the coverter getting more and more generealiasable?

> AGENT

Yes, but with a clear boundary: the core converter is becoming more general for safe, model-agnostic API workflow conversion, while the runner now carries runtime-specific patches for known external-template hazards. The generalizable improvement is the important one: `set_prompt`, `set_seed`, `set_steps`, and `set_model` now only bind to values with the right type. That prevents the converter from corrupting arbitrary nodes whose input names happen to be `positive`, `seed`, etc. The LTX upscaler bypass and Klein 9B GGUF rewrite are not pretending to be universal conversion rules; they’re explicit corpus/runtime adapters for cases where the official template or model access does not run cleanly in this environment. That’s the right direction: keep the converter conservative and general, and keep environment/model-family repairs explicit and test-covered.

> AGENT

Yes, mostly. Current behavior: - Official Comfy templates: model URLs are embedded in the workflow JSON. The matrix runs baseline with `comfyui run-workflow ... --all`, which downloads the referenced models automatically into the expected `models/...` folders. - VibeComfy runs: they reuse those downloaded models from the same pod filesystem after the baseline has warmed them. - Flux 9B GGUF: handled explicitly with `hf_hub_download(...)` because that workflow uses the GGUF custom node and needs the model placed at `models/diffusion_models/flux-2-klein-9b-Q4_K_M.gguf`. - LTX: currently relies on the workflow/model references plus installed LTX custom nodes. This is the part most likely to need explicit model prefetching if the custom-node workflow does not expose standard downloadable model URL metadata cleanly. I think the right design is: yes, pass/store model URLs, but not as ad hoc CLI flags every time. We should make each ready template/corpus manifest carry a `models` section: ```json { "models": [ { "url": "https://huggingface.co/...", "path": "models/diffusion_models/foo.safetensors", "sha256": "optional" } ] } ``` Then `vibecomfy run` / `vibecomfy doctor` can preflight or download missing models deterministically before execution. Raw official JSON can still be auto-extracted, but ready templates should have explicit resolved model requirements so they do not depend on baseline ComfyUI having run first. Current RunPod progress also improved: `wan_i2v` passed, so we now have `qwen_image_edit`, `z_image`, `wan_t2v`, and `wan_i2v` fully `ok`. Flux 4B VibeComfy is running now.

No. It is cleaner than the generated `API_WORKFLOW` blob, but it is not as clean as it can be. The current version is still too close to Comfy internals: ```python node_id="75" subgraph="7b34ab90-..." widget_3=model workflow.connect(f"{text_node.id}.0", f"{image.id}.text") ``` That is Python-shaped graph assembly, not a great user-facing Python workflow. The cleaner target is a small typed builder layer where the template reads like intent: ```python from vibecomfy.templates import flux2_klein from vibecomfy.io import save_image def build(): prompt = ( "A hedgehog wearing a tiny party hat surrounded by confetti, " "2000s digicam aesthetic, flash photography" ) base = flux2_klein.text_to_image( prompt, model="base-4b", width=1024, height=1024, seed=2734, ) tuned = flux2_klein.text_to_image( prompt, model="4b", width=1024, height=1024, seed=2734, ) save_image(base, prefix="Flux2-Klein/base") save_image(tuned, prefix="Flux2-Klein/4b") return [base, tuned] ``` Or, if we want explicit workflow objects: ```python def build(): workflow = Workflow("image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i") prompt = workflow.prompt("...") base = workflow.flux2_klein.t2i(prompt, model="base-4b", size=(1024, 1024)) tuned = workflow.flux2_klein.t2i(prompt, model="4b", size=(1024, 1024)) workflow.save(base, "Flux2-Klein/base") workflow.save(tuned, "Flux2-Klein/4b") return workflow ``` That is the direction I’d take. Key improvements needed: - Hide node ids. Users should not see `"75"` or `"9"` unless debugging. - Hide widget numbers. `widget_3` should become `model`, `widget_4` should become `text_encoder`, etc. - Hide UUID subgraph class names behind named wrappers like `flux2_klein.text_to_image`. - Make outputs first-class Python objects, so `save_image(image)` works without manual `connect()`. - Keep an escape hatch like `workflow.raw_node(...)` for unsupported custom nodes. - Preserve compile-to-Comfy-API as an implementation detail. So the current file is a useful proof of concept, but the maximally composable design needs a real mini-DSL / typed wrapper layer. The first template should probably become an example of that API, not hand-roll helper functions inside every file.

A clean ready template could look like this: ```python from vibecomfy import Workflow from vibecomfy.templates import flux2_klein def build(): wf = Workflow("image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i") prompt = wf.text( "A hedgehog wearing a tiny party hat surrounded by confetti, " "early digital camera style, slight noise, flash photography, " "candid moment, 2000s digicam aesthetic, festive birthday celebration atmosphere" ) base = flux2_klein.text_to_image( wf, prompt, model="base-4b", width=1024, height=1024, seed=2734, ) tuned = flux2_klein.text_to_image( wf, prompt, model="4b", width=1024, height=1024, seed=2734, ) wf.save_image(base, prefix="Flux2-Klein/base") wf.save_image(tuned, prefix="Flux2-Klein/4b") return wf ``` Or even tighter: ```python from vibecomfy import Workflow def build(): wf = Workflow("image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i") prompt = ( "A hedgehog wearing a tiny party hat surrounded by confetti, " "early digital camera style, slight noise, flash photography, " "candid moment, 2000s digicam aesthetic, festive birthday celebration atmosphere" ) wf.flux2_klein.t2i(prompt, model="base-4b").save("Flux2-Klein/base") wf.flux2_klein.t2i(prompt, model="4b").save("Flux2-Klein/4b") return wf ``` For LTX, the same style should scale to video: ```python from vibecomfy import Workflow def build(): wf = Workflow("video/ltx2_3_t2v") video = wf.ltx.t2v( prompt="A quiet cinematic shot of a glass greenhouse in rain", model="ltx-2.3-22b-dev-fp8", width=768, height=512, frames=97, steps=8, seed=42, ) video.save("ltx/t2v") return wf ``` For custom nodes, we still need an escape hatch: ```python motion = wf.raw_node( "KJNodes::SomeCustomNode", image=input_image, strength=0.65, ) ``` The important thing: users compose with Python objects like `prompt`, `image`, `video`, and `.save()`. VibeComfy handles nodes, edges, widget names, UUIDs, and final Comfy API JSON internally.

> DEVELOPER

but why's it still linking a workflow?

> AGENT

Because that field is provenance, not execution. In the converted file: ```python "source_workflow": "workflow_corpus/official/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.json" ``` means: “this Python template was derived from this official Comfy JSON template.” It does **not** mean VibeComfy runs that JSON file at runtime. Runtime path is: ```text ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py -> build() -> VibeWorkflow object -> compile() -> Comfy API dict -> HiddenSwitch queue/run ``` The source JSON is there so we can audit where the template came from, re-diff against upstream official templates, refresh conversions later, and prove we did not invent the workflow blindly. If it’s confusing, we should rename the metadata from: ```python "source_workflow": ... ``` to something clearer like: ```python "derived_from_json": "workflow_corpus/official/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.json" ``` or: ```python "provenance": { "derived_from": "workflow_corpus/official/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.json", "runtime_source": "native_python", } ``` That would make the distinction explicit.

> DEVELOPER

why is it not just all the nodes executed via python for each workflow?

> AGENT

Because Comfy’s runtime model is graph execution, not Python-script execution. Even if the authoring layer is pure Python, Comfy/HiddenSwitch ultimately wants a graph: ```text nodes + inputs + edges -> queue prompt -> executor runs nodes ``` So VibeComfy has two possible meanings of “Python”: 1. **Python as authoring language** You write Python to compose the graph: ```python image = flux.t2i(prompt) image.save("out") ``` Then VibeComfy compiles that to a Comfy prompt graph. This is the right model for us. 2. **Python directly executes each node** Your Python script imports node classes, instantiates them, calls their methods, manages tensors/models/devices/caches, and saves outputs. That is theoretically possible, but it is much harder and mostly throws away Comfy’s executor. Why not execute every node directly in Python? - Comfy nodes are not designed as a stable direct-call API. - Many nodes rely on Comfy’s executor context, model management, folder paths, progress hooks, caching, device placement, interrupt handling, and output routing. - Custom nodes vary wildly. Some are easy to call directly; many assume the Comfy graph runtime. - You would need to reimplement scheduling, memory cleanup, lazy loading, preview handling, model cache behavior, and output collection. - HiddenSwitch already provides the embedded/queue execution path. Bypassing it would create a second runtime. So the right architecture is: ```text pure Python workflow authoring -> VibeComfy graph IR -> Comfy/HiddenSwitch graph execution ``` Not: ```text pure Python workflow authoring -> manually call every Comfy node class ``` The composability should live above Comfy, not by replacing Comfy’s executor.

> DEVELOPER

Does Hiddenswitch not already have a composition layer like this? Or COmfyScript?

> AGENT

I’ll check both the local VibeComfy notes/code and the installed/runtime assumptions. The key distinction I’m looking for is whether an existing layer gives us composable authoring while still compiling to Comfy’s executor, or whether it only serializes/executes an existing graph.

> AGENT

Yes, but neither gives exactly the layer we want. HiddenSwitch has **GraphBuilder**: ```python from comfy_execution.graph_utils import GraphBuilder builder = GraphBuilder(prefix="") builder.node("SaveImage", id="9", images=["75", 0], filename_prefix="Flux2") api = builder.finalize() ``` That is useful, but it is still a low-level graph builder. It hides a little dict construction, but it does not give us: ```python image = wf.flux2_klein.t2i(prompt, model="4b") image.save("Flux2-Klein") ``` So GraphBuilder is a compiler/backend helper, not a nice authoring DSL. ComfyScript is closer. It tries to express Comfy workflows as Python. But our spike in [docs/comfyscript_spike.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/docs/comfyscript_spike.md:1) found problems: - Requires a live Comfy server because it needs `/object_info`. - Failed on `MarkdownNote` in several official templates. - Failed on missing custom nodes in active runtime. - Emits ComfyScript-specific Python, not our `VibeWorkflow` editable IR. - It is not integrated with our ready-template registry, metadata, model requirements, RunPod validation, patches, or doctor flow. So my read is: - **Use HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder optionally** as one compile backend. - **Use ComfyScript as reference/import-export inspiration**, maybe an adapter later. - **Do not make either the core authoring model.** The core should be VibeComfy’s own Python layer: ```python wf = Workflow("image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i") prompt = wf.text("...") image = wf.flux2_klein.t2i(prompt, model="4b", width=1024, height=1024) image.save("Flux2-Klein") return wf ``` Then internally: ```text VibeComfy Python DSL -> VibeWorkflow IR -> compile via direct API dict or HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder -> HiddenSwitch Comfy executor ``` So you’re right to ask. There is prior art, but it does not remove the need for our own clean composable layer.

> DEVELOPER

And what would be the limitations of this approach or drawbacks? I want to ultimately be able to tweak and refine and update workflows in as composable way as possible, and then combine multiple of these together in a flow?

> AGENT

The approach is right for what you want, but the drawbacks are real. **Main Limitation** A Python DSL can make workflows composable, but it still has to compile into Comfy’s graph model. That means composition is only easy when the abstractions expose the right handles: `image`, `latent`, `conditioning`, `video`, `audio`, `model`, `vae`, etc. If a wrapper hides too much, you cannot splice in ControlNet, LoRA, IP-Adapter, or custom preprocessing cleanly. If it exposes too much, it becomes Comfy JSON with nicer syntax. **Key Drawbacks** - **Wrapper coverage becomes the bottleneck.** Every major node family needs a good wrapper: Flux, Z-Image, Qwen edit, Wan, LTX, LoRA, ControlNet, VAE, samplers, video combine, audio, custom nodes. Unknown nodes still need escape hatches. - **Custom nodes are messy.** Kijai, KJNodes, LTXVideo, WanVideoWrapper, etc. often have unstable inputs/classes. A clean DSL needs typed wrappers for common paths and `raw_node(...)` for everything else. - **Subgraphs are awkward.** Official Comfy templates often hide real logic behind UUID subgraph nodes. We can wrap them, but true composability may require unpacking/materializing those subgraphs. - **Graph composition needs typed outputs.** To combine workflows, outputs need semantic types. Example: `ltx.i2v(image=flux_image)` should know that `flux_image` is an `ImageHandle`, not just node `75` output `0`. - **Debugging can get harder if too abstract.** Users need a way to inspect the compiled graph, trace which Python call created which Comfy node, and patch low-level inputs when wrappers are wrong. - **Round-tripping is imperfect.** Imported Comfy JSON -> Python DSL will often be approximate. Some workflows convert beautifully; others become hybrid Python plus `raw_node(...)`. - **Runtime failures still happen at graph execution.** The Python layer can catch schema errors earlier, but missing models, VRAM issues, custom-node bugs, and HiddenSwitch drift still show up when Comfy runs. **Best Shape** I’d design it as layered, not one magic abstraction: ```python wf = Workflow("my_flow") image = wf.flux2.t2i("a product photo", model="4b") edited = wf.qwen.edit(image, "make the background white") video = wf.ltx.i2v(edited, prompt="slow cinematic camera push") audio = wf.ace.song("soft ambient brand music") wf.save_image(edited, "final/edit") wf.save_video(video, "final/video") wf.save_audio(audio, "final/audio") return wf ``` But also allow: ```python wf.patch(video, lora="cinematic_motion.safetensors", strength=0.7) wf.raw_node("SomeCustomNode", input=video, weird_widget=123) api = wf.compile("api") ``` So yes: this is the right path for tweak/refine/update/combine. The trick is building a DSL that is **semantic enough to compose** and **transparent enough to debug**.

> AGENT

Yes, with an important boundary. **Custom nodes on the fly** We should support: ```python wf.require_nodepack("kijai/ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper", ref="pinned-sha") wf.require_nodepack("kijai/ComfyUI-KJNodes", ref="pinned-sha") ``` Then VibeComfy can: 1. Install/sync the custom node repo. 2. Install its requirements. 3. Restart or start the Comfy/HiddenSwitch session with that nodepack loaded. 4. Refresh `/object_info`. 5. Validate and run the workflow. The constraint: Comfy generally discovers custom nodes at startup/import time. So “on the fly” should mean **before a run/session starts**, not hot-swapping arbitrary Python packages into an already-running executor and expecting them to appear safely. For hot-loading, we should assume restart/reload unless proven otherwise. **Mixing Comfy execution with normal Python** Yes, and this is one of the biggest reasons to keep our own Python layer. Example target shape: ```python def build(): wf = Workflow("product_video") source = python.download_image(url) cropped = python.crop_to_square(source) caption = python.describe_image(cropped) image = wf.qwen.edit(cropped, f"make this a premium studio product photo: {caption}") video = wf.ltx.i2v(image, prompt="slow cinematic camera push") audio = python.generate_music_prompt(caption) wf.save_image(image, "final/product") wf.save_video(video, "final/video") return wf ``` But there are two modes: ```text normal Python before/between/after Comfy graph runs = yes normal Python inside a Comfy graph execution = only via custom node or explicit external-python wrapper ``` So the clean architecture is: ```text Python orchestration -> run Python functions -> build/modify Comfy graph -> execute graph through HiddenSwitch -> inspect outputs -> run more Python -> execute another graph ``` Not: ```text Comfy executor magically calls arbitrary local Python mid-graph ``` unless we wrap that Python as a custom node. I added this as an explicit requirement to the active megaplan doc run.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

I’ll fold both of these into the active design doc: custom node pinning needs to be part of the contract, and graph-output-to-Python-to-next-graph is probably the core orchestration pattern rather than an edge case. I’m checking the megaplan critique status before I continue.

> AGENT

Yes on both. For custom nodes, we should pin at the **custom-node package level** first: ```python wf.require_nodepack( "ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper", repo="https://github.com/kijai/ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper.git", ref="8f3a...commit_sha", ) ``` Best hierarchy: ```text nodepack repo URL -> immutable commit SHA -> optional branch/tag label for human readability -> requirements lock/hash if practical -> discovered node classes from /object_info ``` Pinning individual node classes is probably too granular as the default. The stable install unit is the repo/package. But we should record which node classes came from which pinned nodepack so doctor/debug can say: ```text WanVideoSampler came from ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper@8f3a... ``` For combining graph output with Python and passing into another graph: yes, that is probably the best core model. Target shape: ```python def run_flow(): img = run(wf.flux.t2i("a product photo")).image cropped = python.crop(img.path) caption = python.describe(cropped) edited = run(wf.qwen.edit(cropped, f"make this premium: {caption}")).image video = run(wf.ltx.i2v(edited, prompt="slow cinematic camera push")).video return video ``` So the architecture should support: ```text Graph A -> typed output handle -> normal Python -> Graph B ``` This is cleaner than trying to shove arbitrary Python inside the Comfy executor. If Python must run inside the graph, then it should be wrapped as a custom node. Otherwise, the default should be orchestration between graph runs. The main design need is typed handles: ```python ImageHandle(path=...) VideoHandle(path=...) AudioHandle(path=...) LatentHandle(...) ConditioningHandle(...) ``` Then Python functions and graph builders can both accept/return these handles. That gives us the composability you want without fighting Comfy’s executor model.

Practical questions we should force the doc to answer: 1. **What is the execution unit?** Is a “flow” one Comfy graph, or a Python orchestration that can run many Comfy graphs with Python in between? I think: a flow should be Python orchestration; each Comfy graph run is one step. 2. **What are the typed values?** We need first-class handles like `Image`, `Video`, `Audio`, `Latent`, `Conditioning`, `Model`, `Mask`, `Text`. Without these, composition gets messy fast. 3. **Where can arbitrary Python run?** Best answer: - before graph execution - after graph execution - between graph executions - inside graph execution only via custom node / explicit wrapper 4. **How do custom nodes get pinned?** Pin nodepacks by repo + commit SHA. Record discovered node classes from that pinned nodepack. Probably do not pin every node class individually by default. 5. **How do we restart/reload after adding custom nodes?** If custom nodes are added, assume Comfy session restart is required. The plan should not promise hot-loading unless we prove it. 6. **How does debugging work?** `run_until(handle)` sounds nice, but only works once handles have explicit output types. The doc should say typed handles are a prerequisite. 7. **How do we preserve escape hatches?** Need `wf.raw_node(...)` and `wf.from_api_dict(...)` so unsupported custom workflows are still usable. 8. **How do we migrate ready templates?** Not big bang. Keep runtime-green dict-backed templates stable; migrate to native Python when edited or when wrappers exist. The Flux template we just changed is the precedent. 9. **What does provenance mean?** Separate: - `derived_from`: original Comfy JSON/template - `runtime_source`: native Python / imported JSON / raw API - `nodepack_lock`: exact custom-node versions 10. **How do we combine workflows cleanly?** The plan should show: ```python img = run(flux.t2i(...)).image img = python.crop(img) vid = run(ltx.i2v(img, ...)).video ``` That should be treated as the primary model, not an afterthought.

Yes. The current megaplan now looks like it has absorbed the real requirements. It explicitly includes: - Python orchestration as the top-level unit, with multiple Comfy graph executions inside one flow. - Graph -> Python -> graph as the primary composition pattern. - Typed handles as a prerequisite: `Image`, `Video`, `Audio`, `Latent`, `Conditioning`, etc. - Arbitrary Python only before/after/between graph runs by default. - Runtime Python inside a graph only via a real `ExternalPythonNode` / custom node, not a normal authoring `@block`. - Custom node installation before a run, with default session restart/reload. - Custom node pinning by `git_commit_sha`, optional `semantic_label`, and optional per-node-class `source_sha256`. - Raw JSON / raw node / raw API escape hatches. - Manual, iterative ready-template migration, using the Flux native template as the precedent. - Structured provenance: source JSON, runtime source, nodepack locks, authored Python file. - Debug helpers gated on typed handles and schema-backed output types. > DEVELOPER

This like it'll be as beautiful and elegant as possible?

> AGENT

It is pointing in the right direction, but “as beautiful and elegant as possible” depends on one hard constraint: we must avoid letting the Python layer become Comfy JSON with nicer clothes. The elegant version is: ```python flow = Flow("product_launch") image = flow.run( flux.klein.t2i("premium studio photo of a ceramic speaker", model="4b") ).image edited = python.remove_background(image) caption = python.describe(edited) video = flow.run( ltx.i2v(edited, prompt=f"slow cinematic camera push, {caption}") ).video flow.save(video, "final/product_video") ``` The ugly version is: ```python wf.node("SomeClass", widget_0=..., widget_1=...) wf.connect("12.0", "18.image") ``` The plan now recognizes that distinction. To keep it elegant, I’d make these design rules non-negotiable: - Users compose with semantic objects: `Image`, `Video`, `Audio`, `Prompt`, `Model`, `Latent`, not node ids. - Node ids and widget names are debug-only. - Common workflows are one-liners or small functions: `flux.klein.t2i`, `qwen.edit`, `ltx.i2v`, `wan.t2v`. - Escape hatches exist, but they should look deliberately lower-level: `raw_node`, `raw_api`, `from_json`. - Multi-stage flows are normal Python, not a special YAML/graph-on-top abstraction. - Every output can be inspected, saved, transformed by Python, then passed to another graph. - The compiled Comfy graph is always inspectable when something breaks. So yes: the requirements now support an elegant architecture. The execution will only stay elegant if we are disciplined about keeping the user-facing DSL semantic and pushing Comfy’s messy details behind wrappers.

> AGENT

Yes. `docs/python_composition_dsl_review.md` is mostly valid, and it catches several issues the plan should address before we treat it as implementation-ready. The strongest valid critiques: - **North-star API still missing:** matches our own critique. The plan should show the desired user experience before the architecture diagram. - **Abstraction sprawl:** valid. `Template`, `Pipeline`, `Recipe`, `VibeFlow`, `build()` are too many names. I’d keep `VibeFlow` for multi-stage orchestration, `Template` for reusable single-graph builders, and drop/avoid `Recipe`. - **Typed handles are overstated:** valid. `Handle[T]` is not truly statically typed unless we add generated wrappers / overloads / schema-type registry. The doc should say P1 gives typed metadata and lintable handles, not full mypy-grade safety. - **`str(handle)` is risky:** valid. Backward compatibility is necessary, but silent type erasure is a footgun. We need lint/doctor warnings around coercion and raw string refs. - **No-Python-in-graph needs enforcement:** valid. `compile("api")` should reject non-serializable live Python objects unless they are explicitly represented as an `ExternalPythonNode`. - **`VibeFlow` failure semantics are under-specified:** very valid. Cancellation, serialization, run-dir naming, stage identity, and resume behavior need to be settled before P3. - **Custom-node reload needs a real verb:** valid. `session.stop(); install; session.start()` is a recipe, not a contract. We should define `reload_for_nodepack_change()` or equivalent. - **SHA verification is doc-only right now:** valid. Lockfile claims need doctor/runtime checks and a mismatch policy. - **Escape hatches must re-enter composition:** valid. We need `VibeWorkflow.wrap_api_dict(...)` or equivalent so raw API dicts can be a `VibeFlow` stage. - **Flux builder precedent is imperfect:** valid. The current Flux native builder still uses private `node()` helpers, raw IDs, and string `connect()`. It is a transition precedent, not the final beautiful API. Parts I’d soften: - **“Merge Patch into Block”**: maybe not. The distinction is useful conceptually: blocks create handles; patches decorate/splice existing topology. But the doc should admit this is behavioral convention unless enforced by API shape. - **“P1 must add all code-level gates”**: directionally right, but maybe too much for P1. I’d separate “P1 hard gates” from “P1/P2 hardening.” - **“Handle[T] decorative”**: true for static typing, but still valuable for runtime typing, linting, and UX. The plan should be more honest, not abandon it. Net: the review is useful and should drive a revision. The biggest changes: add the north-star API section, collapse terminology, clarify typed-handle limits, add enforcement gates, and promote `VibeFlow` failure semantics from open question to a pre-P3 decision.

I’ll sanity-check the actual plan shape rather than judging from memory, then I’ll give you the critical read: what feels elegant, what still feels overcomplicated, and what a strong engineer would likely push back on.

> AGENT

Yes, directionally it’s elegant. A top-tier engineer would likely say: the architecture is now coherent because it has one canonical IR (`VibeWorkflow`), one friendly orchestration layer (`VibeFlow`), and clear escape hatches instead of pretending every Comfy graph can be made beautiful immediately. The strongest parts: - The “ordinary Python around graph execution” model is the right abstraction. - Keeping raw JSON supported but demoted to import/escape-hatch material is pragmatic. - Typed handles as metadata-first, not fake full static typing, is honest. - Custom nodes are handled through lifecycle, lockfiles, and generic `wf.node(...)`, which avoids an impossible wrapper-everything strategy. - The plan does not confuse authoring-time blocks with runtime Python execution. What a very strong engineer would still push on: - The north-star API is elegant, but the implementation path needs a thin first slice soon. Otherwise it risks becoming architecture poetry. - `Template`, `Block`, `Patch`, `Stage`, `Handle`, `Artifact`, `VibeFlow` is a lot of vocabulary. It is defensible, but the public API should make most users learn only `template -> tweak -> run`. - `ExternalPythonNode` should stay rare and probably delayed. The core product win is Python between graphs, not Python inside graphs. - The Flux builder should be rewritten into the planned handle style before using it as the example others copy. - The plan should probably choose one “golden path” example and make everything else subordinate to that. My read: it’s elegant enough as an architecture plan, but the real test is whether P1 can feel tiny and obvious. A top-tier engineer would approve the direction, then immediately ask for the smallest working API slice that proves the taste.

But there would still be a few intentional loose ends, not accidental ones: - `ExternalPythonNode` may remain documented but only implemented if a real template needs it. - Some raw templates may remain only if they have explicit technical-exception notes. - Static typing would still be runtime/schema-assisted, not full generated mypy-grade wrappers for every node. - ComfyScript would remain import/reference material, not a core authoring path. - Future model families and new custom nodes would still require ongoing template additions and validation. So: no structural loose ends for this phase if executed cleanly. But not “the universe is finished.” It would be a complete, coherent v1 of the architecture with known extension points.

> DEVELOPER

Will new custom nodes and templates and models fit elegantly into this? What would be the best way to have the community contribute them?

> AGENT

Yes, if implemented this way, new custom nodes/templates/models should fit cleanly. The best shape is a **contribution pipeline with layers**: 1. **Raw import layer** Contributors can submit official Comfy JSON, upstream example JSON, or a Python builder. Raw JSON is acceptable as source material, not the final “ready” form. 2. **Normalization layer** The repo converts/imports raw workflows into `VibeWorkflow`, preserving provenance: - source URL/repo - original workflow file - model requirements - custom node requirements - known limitations 3. **Authored template layer** Maintainers or trusted contributors convert the workflow into public Python composition: ```python wf = VibeWorkflow("wan_i2v") image = wf.node(...).out("IMAGE") video = wf.node(...).out("VIDEO") ``` This becomes the reusable template. 4. **Requirements layer** Every template declares: - model files / URLs / Hugging Face IDs - custom nodepacks - pinned commits or lockfile entries - VRAM/runtime expectations - supported input/output types 5. **Validation layer** A submitted template is not “ready” until CI or RunPod validation proves: - schema passes - compiles to Comfy API JSON - doctor passes - required models/nodes resolve - runtime execution succeeds - output artifact is produced 6. **Ready registry** Only validated templates enter something like: ```text ready_templates/ image/ video/ audio/ editing/ control/ ``` Raw imported material stays separate, e.g.: ```text vendor/workflows/ imported_workflows/ ``` For community contributions, I’d make the path explicit: - **Easy mode:** submit raw Comfy workflow + source link + expected output. - **Better mode:** submit raw workflow plus model/custom-node metadata. - **Best mode:** submit a composed Python template plus validation evidence. - **Maintainer gate:** only maintainers mark something `ready`. The important principle: contributors should not need to understand the whole system to contribute, but the repo should never confuse “submitted” with “ready.” That distinction keeps the system elegant as it grows.

> DEVELOPER

ANY OTHER QWEN_IMAGE MODELS TO ADD?

> AGENT

I’ll check what we already have locally, then verify current Qwen Image variants from primary sources so we don’t miss newer official/edit/control/LoRA options.

> AGENT

Yes. We currently only have `qwen_image_edit` checked in as a ready image/edit Qwen template. We should add several more. Priority list: 1. `qwen_image_2512_t2i` - Newer text-to-image foundation model. - Official Comfy doc says it improves realism, detail, and text rendering. - Should use FP8 by default plus optional 4-step Lightning LoRA. 2. `qwen_image_t2i` - Original Qwen-Image base text-to-image. - Useful as baseline coverage and for ControlNet/LoRA composition. 3. `qwen_image_distill_t2i` - Distilled Qwen-Image variant. - Faster/lower-step template, good for smoke/runtime validation. 4. `qwen_image_edit_2511` - Newer edit model. - Official doc says improved character consistency, multi-person editing, geometric reasoning, and integrated LoRA capabilities. - This is probably the most important missing edit template. 5. `qwen_image_layered` - Layer decomposition model: outputs editable RGBA layers. - This is a distinct capability, not just another edit model. 6. `qwen_image_union_control_lora` - Structure-control LoRA for canny/depth/pose/lineart/softedge/normal/openpose. - This gives us a real Qwen “LoRA/control mode”. 7. `qwen_image_diffsynth_controlnet_canny/depth/inpaint` - Model-patch based control workflows. - Worth adding after the simpler base/edit templates because preprocessing and patch nodes add more failure modes. So the clean Qwen Image matrix should be: - T2I: original, 2512, distilled - Edit: edit base, edit 2511 - Control: InstantX/Union LoRA/DiffSynth canny-depth-inpaint - Utility/editing: Layered decomposition - Acceleration: Lightning LoRA variants where official workflows expose them Sources: official Comfy Qwen docs list these workflows and model links: Qwen-Image, Qwen-Image-2512, Qwen-Image-Edit, Qwen-Image-Edit-2511, and Qwen-Image-Layered.

> DEVELOPER

And if i wanted to train a qwen voice how woudl i do that?

> AGENT

For Qwen voice, I’d separate **voice cloning** from **training/fine-tuning**. Most likely you do **not** need to “train” first. Qwen3-TTS supports short-reference voice cloning: give it a clean voice sample and text, and it synthesizes in that voice. Official/Qwen repo descriptions say the Base models support rapid voice cloning from user audio, while CustomVoice and VoiceDesign cover preset/prompted voices and description-based voice creation. Sources: [QwenLM/Qwen3-TTS](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-TTS), [Qwen3-TTS paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15621), [Alibaba Qwen voice cloning docs](https://www.alibabacloud.com/help/en/model-studio/qwen-tts-voice-cloning). The practical path: 1. **Use cloning first** - Record or supply 10-30 seconds of clean speech. - Trim silence/noise, normalize loudness, keep one speaker. - Run Qwen3-TTS clone/custom voice workflow. - In VibeComfy terms, this should become a ready template like `audio/qwen3_tts_voice_clone.py`. 2. **Use VoiceDesign if you want a fictional/new voice** - Describe the voice: age, accent, tone, pace, emotion, timbre. - No reference audio required. - This is better for “make me a narrator voice” than “copy this exact person.” 3. **Fine-tune only if cloning is not stable enough** - Use it when you need a reusable production voice with consistent style over many prompts. - You’d gather a dataset: ideally 20-60+ minutes clean speech, transcript-aligned, one speaker, consistent mic/room. - Slice into short clips, normalize audio, validate transcripts. - Fine-tune the Qwen3-TTS Base model or a supported custom voice training wrapper. - Save the adapter/checkpoint and add it as a model asset in our registry/templates. For our repo, the next useful thing would be: - Add a `qwen_tts_voice_clone` ready template. - Add a `qwen_tts_voice_design` ready template if not already good enough. - Add a `qwen_tts_finetune_dataset_prep` utility/template later. - Document model/input requirements and run it on RunPod with a real sample. Important constraint: only clone/train voices you have rights and consent to use.
